1
implies that
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{A}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}<\frac{1}{2}
, therefore we obtain that

\displaystyle 2\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{A}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{B}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{R^{-1}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\left(1+\frac{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{B}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{R^{-1}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}{1-\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{A}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}}\right)\leq 2\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{A}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{B}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{R^{-1}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\left(1+2\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{B}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{R^{-1}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\right)\text{\,,}

which is precisely the upper bound stated in
Lemma

1
.

Appendix C
Proof of
Theorem

1

In this appendix, we prove
Theorem

1
, namely the comparison between the unsafe/LQR map and the safe/
H_{\infty}
-robust map in the risk-handling setting of
Section

2.3
. Throughout our proof, we focus on state cost matrices that are simultaneously diagonalizable in the basis
V
,
i.e.
, the set of cost matrices given by

\displaystyle\Theta_{V}:=\left\{Q\in\Theta:Q=V\Lambda_{Q}V^{\top}\,,\,\Lambda_{Q}:=\diag(q_{1},\ldots,q_{m})\right\}\text{\,.}

Since
\Theta
is made up of PSD matrices of Frobenius norm at most 1, and since
V
is orthogonal, the set
\Theta_{V}
may be written as

\displaystyle\Theta_{V}=\left\{V\diag(q_{1},\dots,q_{m})V^{\top}:q_{j}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,,\,j\in[m]\,,\,\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{q}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\leq 1\right\}\text{\,.}

We will provide a lower bound on the Lipschitz constant within this set, which is sufficient to conclude the bound holds for all cost matrices in
\Theta
. For readability, throughout this appendix we write

\displaystyle A=V\Lambda_{A}V^{\top},\qquad B=V\Lambda_{B}V^{\top},\qquad D=V\Lambda_{D}V^{\top},\qquad R=V\Lambda_{R}V^{\top}\text{\,,}

where
\Lambda_{A}=\diag(a_{1},\dots,a_{m})
,
\Lambda_{B}=\diag(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})
,
\Lambda_{D}=\diag(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
, and
\Lambda_{R}=\diag(r_{1},\dots,r_{m})
are diagonal matrices holding the eigenvalues of
A
,
B
,
D
, and
R
in the shared eigenbasis
V
, respectively.

The outline of the proof is as follows.

Section

C.1
shows that under simultaneous orthogonal diagonalizability of
2
, the safe/
H_{\infty}
-robust control problem can be solved through individual scalar
H_{\infty}
-robust control problems (one for each coordinate).

Section

C.2
proceeds to solve these problems and shows that stacking the scalar solutions yields an explicit diagonal mapping that is optimal for the original problem.

Section

C.3
then compares this stacked mapping with the canonical DGARE-synthesized safe mapping we study (defined in
Section

A.2
), and shows that they could partially coincide in a neighborhood of a state cost matrix.
Based on this local coincidence,
Section

C.4
provides a lower bound on the Lipschitz constant of the safe mapping
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(\cdot)
.

Section

C.5
concludes by translating the bounds introduced in
Lemmas

1
and
C.4
to the inequality of
Theorem

1
.

C.1
Simultaneously Diagonalizable
H_{\infty}
-Robust Control

We begin by showing that in the simultaneously diagonalizable setting, state cost matrices in
\Theta_{V}
admit unique values of the safe mapping synthesized via the DGARE (
Equation

20
) that are also simultaneously diagonalizable in the basis
V
.

Proposition 1
.

For any
Q\in\Theta_{V}
the DGARE-synthesized matrix
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
(
Equation

20
) is simultaneously diagonalizable in the basis
V
, i.e.,
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)=V\Lambda_{K}(Q)V^{\top}
where
\Lambda_{K}(Q)\in\mathbb{R}^{m,m}
is a diagonal matrix denoted as

\displaystyle\Lambda_{K}(Q)=:\diag(k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{1}(Q),\dots,k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{m}(Q))\text{\,.}

Proof.

Let
\gamma\geq\gamma_{\infty}(Q)
.
Recall that the matrices
\Lambda_{A}=V^{\top}AV
,
\Lambda_{B}=V^{\top}BV
,
\Lambda_{D}=V^{\top}DV
and
\Lambda_{R}=V^{\top}RV
are diagonal, and for any symmetric matrix
P\in\mathbb{R}^{m,m}
, define
\widetilde{P}:=V^{\top}PV
.
For any symmetric matrix
P\in\mathbb{R}^{m,m}
denote

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma):=\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda_{R}+\Lambda_{B}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{B}&\Lambda_{B}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{D}\\
~\\
\Lambda_{D}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{B}&\Lambda_{D}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{D}-\gamma^{2}I\end{pmatrix}

and similarly define
\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P}):=\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda_{B}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A}\\
~\\
\Lambda_{D}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A}\end{pmatrix}\text{\,.}

We have that

\displaystyle H(P;\gamma)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}R+B^{\top}PB&B^{\top}PD\\
D^{\top}PB&D^{\top}PD-\gamma^{2}I\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}V\Lambda_{R}V^{\top}+V\Lambda_{B}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{B}V^{\top}&V\Lambda_{B}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{D}V^{\top}\\
~\\
V\Lambda_{D}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{B}V^{\top}&V\Lambda_{D}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{D}V^{\top}-\gamma^{2}VIV^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}V&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda_{R}+\Lambda_{B}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{B}&\Lambda_{B}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{D}\\
~\\
\Lambda_{D}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{B}&\Lambda_{D}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{D}-\gamma^{2}I\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}V^{\top}&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}V&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma)\begin{pmatrix}V^{\top}&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

where
{\mathbf{0}}
denotes the all-zeros matrix of appropriate dimensions.
Similarly, we have that

\displaystyle F(P)=\begin{pmatrix}B^{\top}PA\\
D^{\top}PA\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}V\Lambda_{B}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A}V^{\top}\\
V\Lambda_{D}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A}V^{\top}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}V&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda_{B}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A}\\
~\\
\Lambda_{D}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A}\end{pmatrix}V^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}V&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})V^{\top}\text{\,.}

Therefore, whenever
H(P;\gamma)
is nonsingular we have by orthogonality of
V
that

\displaystyle H(P;\gamma)^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}V&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma)^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}V^{\top}&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\text{\,.}

Hence, multiplying both sides of the quadratic Riccati term by
V^{\top}
from the left and
V
from the right respectively yields

\displaystyle V^{\top}F(P)^{\top}H(P;\gamma)^{-1}F(P)V

\displaystyle=V^{\top}F(P)^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}V&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma)^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}V^{\top}&{\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{0}}&V^{\top}\end{pmatrix}F(P)V

\displaystyle=\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})^{\top}\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma)^{-1}\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})\text{\,.}

Because
V
is orthogonal, a matrix
P\in\mathbb{R}^{m,m}
satisfies the DGARE (
Equation

18
) if and only if
\widetilde{P}
satisfies the DGARE in the transformed coordinates, obtained by multiplying both sides of
Equation

18
by
V^{\top}
from the left and
V
from the right respectively:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}=\Lambda_{A}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A}+\Lambda_{Q}-\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})^{\top}\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma)^{-1}\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})\text{\,.}

(22)

Consider the set of diagonal matrices
{\mathcal{D}}:=\{\widetilde{P}\in\mathbb{R}^{m,m}:\widetilde{P}\text{ is diagonal}\}
.
We claim that the DGARE operator in
Equation

22
preserves
{\mathcal{D}}
:
if
\widetilde{P}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, then the right-hand side of
Equation

22
is in
{\mathcal{D}}
.
Indeed, since
\Lambda_{A},\Lambda_{B},\Lambda_{D},\Lambda_{Q},\Lambda_{R}
are diagonal, the matrix
\Lambda_{A}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A}+\Lambda_{Q}
is diagonal.
Write the diagonal blocks of
\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma)
as

\displaystyle M:=\Lambda_{R}+\Lambda_{B}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{B},\qquad N:=\Lambda_{B}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{D},\qquad L:=\Lambda_{D}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{D}-\gamma^{2}I\text{\,.}

These matrices are diagonal, hence they commute.
The commutative block-inverse formula gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma)^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}L\Delta^{-1}&-N\Delta^{-1}\\
-N\Delta^{-1}&M\Delta^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\text{\,,}

where
\Delta:=ML-N^{2}
.
Define the diagonal matrices

\displaystyle G:=\Lambda_{B}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A},\qquad J:=\Lambda_{D}^{\top}\widetilde{P}\Lambda_{A},\text{\,.}

Since
\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})=\begin{pmatrix}G\\
J\end{pmatrix}
, unfolding the multiplication gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})^{\top}\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma)^{-1}\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}G^{\top}&J^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}L\Delta^{-1}&-N\Delta^{-1}\\
-N\Delta^{-1}&M\Delta^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}G\\
J\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=G^{\top}L\Delta^{-1}G-G^{\top}N\Delta^{-1}J-J^{\top}N\Delta^{-1}G+J^{\top}M\Delta^{-1}J\text{\,.}

Each term in the final expression is a product of diagonal matrices, hence
\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})^{\top}\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P};\gamma)^{-1}\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P})
is diagonal.
This proves invariance: the DGARE map of
Equation

22
maps
{\mathcal{D}}
to itself.
Now, by assumption there exists a unique stabilizing PSD solution
P_{\infty}(Q;\gamma)
to the DGARE of
Equation

18
.
Equivalently, there exists a unique stabilizing PSD solution
\widetilde{P}_{\infty}:=V^{\top}P_{\infty}(Q;\gamma)V
to
Equation

22
.
Therefore by standard results on the convergence of DGAREs
[
33
,
1
]
, repeated application of the DGARE in
Equation

22
starting from
P={\mathbf{0}}
converges to
\widetilde{P}_{\infty}
.
This together with the invariance of
{\mathcal{D}}
under the DGARE map implies that
\widetilde{P}_{\infty}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,
i.e.
the matrix
V^{\top}P_{\infty}(Q;\gamma)V
is diagonal.
It remains to show that the corresponding
\widetilde{K}_{u}(Q;\gamma)
(
Equation

19
) is diagonal in the same basis.
Evaluate
-\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P}_{\infty};\gamma)^{-1}\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P}_{\infty})
using the same block inverse:

\displaystyle-\widetilde{H}(\widetilde{P}_{\infty};\gamma)^{-1}\widetilde{F}(\widetilde{P}_{\infty})=-\begin{pmatrix}L^{\infty}(\Delta^{\infty})^{-1}G^{\infty}-N^{\infty}(\Delta^{\infty})^{-1}J^{\infty}\\
-N^{\infty}(\Delta^{\infty})^{-1}G^{\infty}+M^{\infty}(\Delta^{\infty})^{-1}J^{\infty}\end{pmatrix}\text{\,,}

where the superscript
\infty
denotes evaluation at
\widetilde{P}_{\infty}=\diag(p_{1}^{\infty},\dots,p_{m}^{\infty})
.
The matrix
\widetilde{K}_{u}(Q;\gamma)
is the upper block of this stacked matrix.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{u}(Q;\gamma)=-\left(L^{\infty}(\Delta^{\infty})^{-1}G^{\infty}-N^{\infty}(\Delta^{\infty})^{-1}J^{\infty}\right)\text{\,.}

Since
L^{\infty},\Delta^{\infty},G^{\infty},N^{\infty},
and
J^{\infty}
are diagonal, the above is a sum of products of diagonal matrices hence it is diagonal.
Transforming back gives
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q;\gamma)=V\widetilde{K}_{u}(Q;\gamma)V^{\top}
, hence
V^{\top}K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q;\gamma)V
is diagonal.
Namely, this holds for
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)=K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q;\gamma_{\infty}(Q))
as required.
∎

Proposition

1
implies that when restricting to the simultaneously diagonalizable set
\Theta_{V}
, the mapping
Q\mapsto K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
can be viewed in the diagonal basis as the mapping

\displaystyle{\mathbf{q}}\mapsto{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}})\text{\,,}

(23)

where for any
Q=V\diag({\mathbf{q}})V^{\top}\in\Theta_{V}
we define
{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}})\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
through

\displaystyle K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)=V\diag({\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}))V^{\top}\text{\,.}

We proceed to showing that the Lipschitz behavior in the original coordinates is bounded by the Lipschitz behavior of this diagonal-basis mapping. For completion, we first define the notion of Lipschitz constant for a mapping between vectors.

Definition 5
.

Let
{\mathcal{V}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
and let
f:{\mathcal{V}}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
be some function. We denote the
Lipschitz constant
of
f
as
\text{Lip}(f)
, given by

\displaystyle\text{Lip}(f):=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{v}}_{1},{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\in{\mathcal{V}}\,,\,{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\neq{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{f({\mathbf{v}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{v}}_{1}-{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}\text{\,.}

Lemma 8
.

Consider the set
{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}_{\geq 0}
given by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}:=\left\{{\mathbf{q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}_{\geq 0}:\ \mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{q}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\leq 1\right\}\text{\,.}

Let
{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}:{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
be the mapping

\displaystyle{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}})=\left(k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{1}({\mathbf{q}}),\dots,k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{m}({\mathbf{q}})\right)\text{\,,}

where
k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}})
is the
j
-th diagonal entry of the matrix
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(\cdot)
in the diagonal basis
V
as in
Equation

23
, so that for any
Q=V\diag({\mathbf{q}})V^{\top}\in\Theta_{V}
,

\displaystyle K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)=V\diag({\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}))V^{\top}\text{\,.}

Let
Q^{(0)}\in\Theta_{V}
and write
Q^{(0)}=V\diag({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)})V^{\top}
with
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
.
For any
j\in[m]
, let
g_{j}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be the function defined by

\displaystyle g_{j}(h)=k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j})\text{\,.}

For any
j\in[m]
, if
q_{j}^{(0)}\in(0,1)
and
g_{j}(\cdot)
is differentiable at
h=0
, then the Lipschitz constant of
K_{\mathrm{safe}}:\Theta_{V}\to\mathbb{R}^{m,m}
satisfies

\displaystyle\text{Lip}(K_{\mathrm{safe}})\geq|g_{j}^{\prime}(0)|\text{\,.}

Proof.

For any
Q^{(1)},Q^{(2)}\in\Theta_{V}
with
Q^{(i)}=V\diag({\mathbf{q}}^{(i)})V^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{q}}^{(i)}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
, orthogonality of
V
implies that

\displaystyle\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q^{(1)})-K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q^{(2)})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{F}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\diag({\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(1)}))-\diag({\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(2)}))}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{F}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(1)})-{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(2)})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\text{\,,}

and similarly
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{Q^{(1)}-Q^{(2)}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{F}=\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{q}}^{(1)}-{\mathbf{q}}^{(2)}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}
.
Hence by
Definition

5

\displaystyle\text{Lip}(K_{\mathrm{safe}})

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{Q^{(1)},Q^{(2)}\in\Theta_{V}\,,\,Q^{(1)}\neq Q^{(2)}}\frac{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q^{(1)})-K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q^{(2)})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{F}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{Q^{(1)}-Q^{(2)}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{F}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{{\mathbf{q}}^{(1)},{\mathbf{q}}^{(2)}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}\,,\,{\mathbf{q}}^{(1)}\neq{\mathbf{q}}^{(2)}}\frac{\|{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(1)})-{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(2)})\|_{2}}{\|{\mathbf{q}}^{(1)}-{\mathbf{q}}^{(2)}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle=\text{Lip}({\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}})\text{\,.}

The fact that
q_{j}^{(0)}\in(0,1)
implies that
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
for all sufficiently small
h\in\mathbb{R}
.
Using the definition of
\text{Lip}({\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}})
and restricting the supremum to the pair
{\mathbf{q}}^{(1)}={\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j}
and
{\mathbf{q}}^{(2)}={\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
, we obtain for all sufficiently small
h\neq 0
that

\displaystyle\text{Lip}({\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}})\geq\frac{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j})-{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)})}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j})-{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}\geq\frac{\left|k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j})-k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)})\right|}{|h|}\text{\,.}

Taking
h\to 0
and using differentiability
g_{j}(\cdot)
at
0
yields

\displaystyle\text{Lip}({\mathbf{k}}^{\mathrm{safe}})

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{h\to 0}\frac{\left|k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j})-k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)})\right|}{|h|}

\displaystyle=\lim_{h\to 0}\frac{|g_{j}(h)-g_{j}(0)|}{|h|}

\displaystyle=|g_{j}^{\prime}(0)|\text{\,.}

as required.
∎

C.2
Scalar Solutions and Stacked Mapping

We now turn to the individual scalar
H_{\infty}
-robust control problems.
In this subsection, we characterize the scalar solution in each coordinate
k_{j}^{*}(\cdot)
and the corresponding scalar optimal value
\Gamma_{j}^{*}(\cdot)
.
We then show that the mapping obtained by stacking these scalar minimizers, denoted
K^{*}(\cdot)
, solves the
H_{\infty}
-robust control problem.
Although potentially different from the mapping
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(\cdot)
synthesized by the coupled block DGARE, the former admits a closed form expression allowing for concrete analysis.
We will later relate it to the coupled block DGARE mapping we are interested in.

We begin by formulating the closed form expression for the scalar objective. For completeness, we first provide the classical definition of the discrete-time Fourier transform which is used in the proof.

Definition 6
.

Let
\{z_{t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
be a sequence of complex numbers such that
\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}|z_{t}|^{2}<\infty
.
We define the
discrete-time Fourier transform
of
\{z_{t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
as the function
\hat{z}:[0,2\pi]\to\mathbb{C}
given by

\displaystyle\hat{z}(\omega)=\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}z_{t}e^{-i\omega t}\text{\,.}

Lemma 9
.

Fix
j\in[m]
.
For any
q_{j}\in[0,1]
and
k_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1
it holds that the robust scalar objective in coordinate
j
is given by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j}):=\sup_{w_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}q_{j}x_{t}^{2}+r_{j}(k_{j}x_{t})^{2}}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}w_{t}^{2}}=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+r_{j}k_{j}^{2}}{(1-|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|)^{2}}\text{\,,}

where the state
x_{t}
is given by
x_{t+1}=a_{j}x_{t}+b_{j}k_{j}x_{t}+d_{j}w_{t}
with
x_{0}=0
.

Proof.

Denote
\xi:=a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}
. For
t\in\mathbb{N}
define
h_{t}:=\xi^{t-1}d_{j}
, and additionally define
h_{0}:=0
.
For any
t\in\mathbb{N}
, unrolling of the state
x_{t}
yields a standard convolution expression of the form

\displaystyle x_{t}=\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\xi^{t-1-\tau}d_{j}w_{\tau}=\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}h_{t-\tau}w_{\tau}\text{\,.}

Observe that since
|\xi|<1
it holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}|h_{t}|=\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}|\xi^{t-1}d_{j}|=\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}|\xi|^{t-1}|d_{j}|=\frac{|d_{j}|}{1-|\xi|}<\infty

and

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}h_{t}^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}(\xi^{t-1}d_{j})^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\xi^{2(t-1)}d_{j}^{2}=\frac{d_{j}^{2}}{1-\xi^{2}}<\infty\text{\,.}

Let
\{w_{t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
be a sequence of real numbers with
\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}w_{t}^{2}<\infty
.
Viewing the real sequences
\{x_{t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
,
\{h_{t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
, and
\{w_{t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
as complex-valued sequences when applying the Fourier-analytic results from
Appendix

D
,
Lemma

16
gives that the discrete-time Fourier transform of
\{x_{t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is the function
\hat{x}:[0,2\pi]\to\mathbb{C}
given by

\displaystyle\hat{x}(\omega)=\hat{h}(\omega)\hat{w}(\omega)\text{\,.}

by
Definition

6
it holds that

\displaystyle\hat{h}(\omega)=\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\xi^{t-1}d_{j}e^{-i\omega t}=d_{j}e^{-i\omega}\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\xi^{t-1}e^{-i\omega(t-1)}=\frac{d_{j}e^{-i\omega}}{1-\xi e^{-i\omega}}=\frac{d_{j}}{e^{i\omega}-\xi}\text{\,.}

Since
|\xi|<1
it holds that the function
f:[0,2\pi]\to\mathbb{R}
given by

\displaystyle f(\omega):=|\hat{h}(\omega)|^{2}=\frac{d_{j}^{2}}{\left|e^{i\omega}-\xi\right|^{2}}

is continuous on
[0,2\pi]
.
Hence, by
Theorem

2
it holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}x_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\hat{x}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\hat{h}(\omega)|^{2}|\hat{w}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sup_{\omega\in[0,2\pi]}f(\omega)\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\hat{w}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle=\left(\sup_{\omega\in[0,2\pi]}f(\omega)\right)\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}w_{t}^{2}\text{\,.}

This implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}x_{t}^{2}}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}w_{t}^{2}}\leq\sup_{\omega\in[0,2\pi]}f(\omega)\text{\,.}

On the other hand, by
Theorem

2
it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}x_{t}^{2}}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}w_{t}^{2}}=\frac{\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(\omega)|\hat{w}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}{\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\hat{w}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}=\frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(\omega)|\hat{w}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}{\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\hat{w}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}

Let
\omega^{*}\in[0,2\pi]
be such that
f(\omega^{*})=\sup_{\omega\in[0,2\pi]}f(\omega)
(exists since
f(\cdot)
is continuous on
[0,2\pi]
).
Since
|\xi|<1
and
|e^{i\omega}|=1
for all
\omega\in[0,2\pi]
it holds by the reverse triangle inequality that

\displaystyle|e^{i\omega}-\xi|\geq||e^{i\omega}|-|\xi||=1-|\xi|

with equality achieved at
\omega=0
when
\xi\geq 0
and at
\omega=\pi
when
\xi<0
.
Using the notation of
Lemma

17
, we can thus take
\omega^{*}\in\{0,\pi\}
so the sequences given by the Lemma are real-valued.
It states that there exists a series of real-valued sequences
\left\{\left\{w_{t}^{(n)}\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
whose discrete-time Fourier transforms
\left\{\hat{w}^{(n)}(\cdot)\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
satisfy

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(\omega)|\hat{w}^{(n)}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}{\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\hat{w}^{(n)}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}=f(\omega^{*})\text{\,.}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{w_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}x_{t}^{2}}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}w_{t}^{2}}=\sup_{w_{t}}\frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(\omega)|\hat{w}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}{\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\hat{w}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}\geq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(\omega)|\hat{w}^{(n)}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}{\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\hat{w}^{(n)}(\omega)|^{2}d\omega}=f(\omega^{*})\text{\,.}

Combining the two inequalities, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{w_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}x_{t}^{2}}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}w_{t}^{2}}=f(\omega^{*})\text{\,.}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{w_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}x_{t}^{2}}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}w_{t}^{2}}=\frac{d_{j}^{2}}{\min_{\omega\in[0,2\pi]}\left|e^{i\omega}-\xi\right|^{2}}=\frac{d_{j}^{2}}{(1-|\xi|)^{2}}\text{\,.}

The proof concludes by noting that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})=(q_{j}+r_{j}k_{j}^{2})\sup_{w_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}x_{t}^{2}}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}w_{t}^{2}}

and plugging back
\xi=a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}
.
∎

Having characterized the robust scalar objective, we are now ready to characterize the solution to the robust scalar control problem in coordinate
j
for a given cost parameter
q_{j}
.

Proposition 2
.

Fix
j\in[m]
. Assume that
a_{j}\neq 0
and
b_{j}\neq 0
.
For any
q_{j}\in[0,\frac{|a_{j}|r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}{b_{j}^{2}})
the robust scalar objective
\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})
from
Lemma

9
has a unique minimizer over the interval

\displaystyle\left\{k_{j}\in\mathbb{R}:|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1\right\}

given by

\displaystyle k_{j}^{*}(q_{j})=-\frac{a_{j}}{|a_{j}|}\frac{b_{j}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}q_{j}\text{\,.}

Additionally, the corresponding optimal scalar value

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j}):=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{j}\in\mathbb{R}\\
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1\end{subarray}}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})=\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j}^{*}(q_{j}))\text{\,,}

satisfies

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})=\frac{d_{j}^{2}}{(1-|a_{j}|)^{2}}\cdot\frac{q_{j}}{1+\frac{b_{j}^{2}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)^{2}}q_{j}}\text{\,.}

In particular, the mapping
q_{j}\mapsto\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
is continuous on the interval
[0,\frac{|a_{j}|r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}{b_{j}^{2}})
.

Proof.

Define the scalar quantity
\zeta_{j}:=b_{j}k_{j}
.
Since
b_{j}\neq 0
, minimizing over
k_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
with
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1
is equivalent to minimizing over
\zeta_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
with
|a_{j}+\zeta_{j}|<1
.
Define
s_{j}:=\frac{a_{j}}{|a_{j}|}\in\{-1,1\}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}:=-s_{j}\zeta_{j}
.
By
Lemma

9
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+r_{j}k_{j}^{2}}{(1-|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|)^{2}}

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\zeta_{j}^{2}}{(1-|a_{j}+\zeta_{j}|)^{2}}

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}s_{j}^{2}\zeta_{j}^{2}}{(1-|s_{j}a_{j}+s_{j}\zeta_{j}|)^{2}}

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{2}}{(1-||a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}|)^{2}}\text{\,.}

Therefore, minimizing over the interval
\zeta_{j}\in(-a_{j}-1,-a_{j}+1)
is equivalent to minimizing over the interval
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}\in(|a_{j}|-1,|a_{j}|+1)
.
We analyze the above expression on the two branches
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}\leq|a_{j}|
and
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}>|a_{j}|
.
On the branch
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}\leq|a_{j}|
, it holds that
||a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}|=|a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}
, hence

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{2}}{(1-|a_{j}|+\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})^{2}}\text{\,.}

Differentiating with respect to
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Gamma_{j}}{\partial\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}}

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\cdot\frac{2\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}(1-|a_{j}|+\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})^{2}-2(q_{j}+\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{2})(1-|a_{j}|+\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})}{(1-|a_{j}|+\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})^{4}}

\displaystyle=2d_{j}^{2}\cdot\frac{\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}(1-|a_{j}|+\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})-(q_{j}+\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{2})}{(1-|a_{j}|+\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})^{3}}

\displaystyle=2d_{j}^{2}\cdot\frac{\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)-q_{j}}{(1-|a_{j}|+\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})^{3}}\text{\,.}

Since
1-|a_{j}|+\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}>0
on this branch, the unique critical point is

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{*}(q_{j})=\frac{b_{j}^{2}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}q_{j}\text{\,,}

and moreover

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Gamma_{j}}{\partial\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}}<0\iff\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}<\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\frac{\partial\Gamma_{j}}{\partial\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}}>0\iff\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}>\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Therefore, within the branch
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}\leq|a_{j}|
, the function has a unique minimizer at
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
.
Since

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{*}(q_{j})<|a_{j}|\iff\frac{b_{j}^{2}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}q_{j}<|a_{j}|\iff q_{j}<\frac{|a_{j}|r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}{b_{j}^{2}}\text{\,,}

the assumed condition on
q_{j}
implies that
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\in(|a_{j}|-1,|a_{j}|]
.
As for the branch
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}>|a_{j}|
, it holds that
||a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}|=\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}-|a_{j}|
, hence

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{2}}{(1+|a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})^{2}}\text{\,.}

Differentiating with respect to
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Gamma_{j}}{\partial\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}}

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\cdot\frac{2\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}(1+|a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})^{2}+2(q_{j}+\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{2})(1+|a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})}{(1+|a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})^{4}}

\displaystyle=2d_{j}^{2}\cdot\frac{\frac{r_{j}}{b_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}(1+|a_{j}|)+q_{j}}{(1+|a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j})^{3}}\text{\,.}

Since
q_{j}>0
,
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}>|a_{j}|>0
and
1+|a_{j}|-\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}>0
on this branch, it follows that
\frac{\partial\Gamma_{j}}{\partial\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}}>0
for all
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}\in(|a_{j}|,|a_{j}|+1)
.
Thus the objective is strictly increasing on the branch
\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}>|a_{j}|
, and therefore has no minimizer there.
Combining the two branches, the global minimizer over
\{k_{j}\in\mathbb{R}:|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1\}
is attained at

\displaystyle k_{j}^{*}(q_{j})=-\frac{s_{j}\widetilde{\zeta}_{j}^{*}(q_{j})}{b_{j}}=-\frac{a_{j}}{|a_{j}|}\frac{b_{j}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}q_{j}\text{\,.}

Consequently,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j}^{*}(q_{j}))

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+r_{j}\left(-\frac{a_{j}}{|a_{j}|}\frac{b_{j}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}q_{j}\right)^{2}}{\left(1-\left|a_{j}+b_{j}\left(-\frac{a_{j}}{|a_{j}|}\frac{b_{j}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}q_{j}\right)\right|\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+\frac{b_{j}^{2}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)^{2}}q_{j}^{2}}{\left((1-|a_{j}|)+\frac{b_{j}^{2}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)}q_{j}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}\left(1+\frac{b_{j}^{2}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)^{2}}q_{j}\right)}{(1-|a_{j}|)^{2}\left(1+\frac{b_{j}^{2}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)^{2}}q_{j}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{j}^{2}}{(1-|a_{j}|)^{2}}\cdot\frac{q_{j}}{1+\frac{b_{j}^{2}}{r_{j}(1-|a_{j}|)^{2}}q_{j}}\text{\,.}

The stated continuity of
q_{j}\mapsto\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
follows immediately from this explicit formula.
∎

We now turn to showing that the mapping obtained by stacking the scalar minimizers for each coordinate,
K^{*}(\cdot)
, satisfies the robust objective.
We begin by proving that for any controller matrix
K
that is diagonal in the basis
V
, the robust objective of
Equation

13
is equal to the maximum of the scalar robust objectives.

Proposition 3
.

Suppose
2
holds. Let
Q=V\diag({\mathbf{q}})V^{\top}\in\Theta_{V}
with
{\mathbf{q}}=(q_{1},\dots,q_{m})
, and let
K=V\diag({\mathbf{k}})V^{\top}
with
{\mathbf{k}}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{m})
.
Consider the linear policy
{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}):=K{\mathbf{x}}
, and assume that for every
j\in[m]
it holds that
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1
.
Then the normalized robust objective of
Equation

13
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\top}Q{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})^{\top}R{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}}=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\text{\,,}

where
\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})
is the scalar robust objective from
Lemma

9
.

Proof.

For any
t\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
, define the transformed vectors
\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t}:=V^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}
and
\widetilde{{\mathbf{w}}}_{t}:=V^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{t}
, and also define the transformed policy
\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}})
through

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}):=V^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}})=V^{\top}K{\mathbf{x}}=V^{\top}V\diag({\mathbf{k}})V^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}=\diag({\mathbf{k}})\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}\text{\,.}

Since
V
is orthogonal and
A,B,D,Q,R,K
are all diagonal in the basis
V
, the disturbed dynamics together with the policy
{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot)
become coordinate-wise:

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=V^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=V^{\top}A{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+V^{\top}B{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})+V^{\top}D{\mathbf{w}}_{t}

\displaystyle=V^{\top}V\Lambda_{A}V^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+V^{\top}V\Lambda_{B}V^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})+V^{\top}V\Lambda_{D}V^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{A}\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t}+\Lambda_{B}\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t})+\Lambda_{D}\widetilde{{\mathbf{w}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{A}\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t}+\Lambda_{B}\diag({\mathbf{k}})\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t}+\Lambda_{D}\widetilde{{\mathbf{w}}}_{t}\text{\,,}

with
\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{0}=0
.
Hence each coordinate
j\in[m]
evolves independently according only to its own disturbance sequence
\{\widetilde{w}_{j,t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
.
Moreover, orthogonality of
V
implies that the disturbance energy is preserved and the quadratic cost separates across coordinates:

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\widetilde{{\mathbf{w}}}_{t}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{w}_{j,t}^{2}\text{\,,}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\top}Q{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+{\mathbf{u}}_{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})^{\top}R{\mathbf{u}}_{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t}^{\top}\diag({\mathbf{q}})\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t}+\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t})^{\top}\Lambda_{R}\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left(q_{j}\widetilde{x}_{j,t}^{2}+r_{j}\widetilde{u}_{j,t}(\widetilde{x}_{j,t})^{2}\right)\text{\,.}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\top}Q{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})^{\top}R{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}}=\sup_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{w}}}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left(q_{j}\widetilde{x}_{j,t}^{2}+r_{j}\widetilde{u}_{j,t}(\widetilde{x}_{j,t})^{2}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{w}_{j,t}^{2}}\text{\,.}

We first prove the right-hand side upper bounds the left-hand side. Let
\{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
be a non-zero disturbance sequence. For each
j\in[m]
, the
j
th coordinate depends only on
(q_{j},k_{j},a_{j},b_{j},d_{j},r_{j})
and on the scalar disturbance sequence
\{\widetilde{w}_{j,t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
. Therefore by
Lemma

9
it holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}q_{j}\widetilde{x}_{j,t}^{2}+r_{j}\widetilde{u}_{j,t}(\widetilde{x}_{j,t})^{2}\leq\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{w}_{j,t}^{2}\text{\,.}

Summing over
j
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left(q_{j}\widetilde{x}_{j,t}^{2}+r_{j}\widetilde{u}_{j,t}(\widetilde{x}_{j,t})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{w}_{j,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\right)\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{w}_{j,t}^{2}

Hence, for every disturbance sequence,

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\top}Q{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})^{\top}R{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left(q_{j}\widetilde{x}_{j,t}^{2}+r_{j}\widetilde{u}_{j,t}(\widetilde{x}_{j,t})^{2}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{w}_{j,t}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\text{\,,}

and taking the supremum over
{\mathbf{w}}_{t}
gives

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\top}Q{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+{\mathbf{u}}_{t}^{\top}R{\mathbf{u}}_{t}}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}}\leq\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\text{\,.}

We now prove the matching lower bound. Let
j^{*}\in[m]
be an index attaining the maximum, that is,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}(q_{j^{*}},k_{j^{*}})=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\text{\,.}

Restrict attention to disturbance sequences satisfying
\widetilde{w}_{j,t}=0
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and
j\neq j^{*}
.
For such disturbances all state coordinates except
j^{*}
remain identically zero: Indeed, for any
j\neq j^{*}
and any
t\in\mathbb{N}
, the
j
th coordinate satisfies

\displaystyle\widetilde{x}_{j,t+1}

\displaystyle=a_{j}\widetilde{x}_{j,t}+b_{j}k_{j}\widetilde{x}_{j,t}+d_{j}\widetilde{w}_{j,t}

\displaystyle=(a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j})\widetilde{x}_{j,t}

\displaystyle=(a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j})^{t+1}\widetilde{x}_{j,0}

\displaystyle=0\text{\,,}

where the last equality follows from the initial condition
\widetilde{x}_{j,0}=0
.
Therefore the vector objective reduces exactly to the scalar objective in coordinate
j^{*}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\top}Q{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})^{\top}R{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}}=\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left(q_{j^{*}}\widetilde{x}_{j^{*},t}^{2}+r_{j^{*}}\widetilde{u}_{j^{*}}(\widetilde{x}_{j^{*},t})^{2}\right)}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{w}_{j^{*},t}^{2}}\text{\,.}

Taking the supremum over disturbances supported on the
j^{*}
th coordinate and using
Lemma

9
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\top}Q{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+{\mathbf{u}}_{t}^{\top}R{\mathbf{u}}_{t}}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{w}}}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left(q_{j}\widetilde{x}_{j,t}^{2}+r_{j}\widetilde{u}_{j,t}(\widetilde{x}_{j,t})^{2}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{w}_{j,t}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{\widetilde{w}_{j^{*},t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left(q_{j^{*}}\widetilde{x}_{j^{*},t}^{2}+r_{j^{*}}\widetilde{u}_{j^{*}}(\widetilde{x}_{j^{*},t})^{2}\right)}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{w}_{j^{*},t}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{j^{*}}(q_{j^{*}},k_{j^{*}})

\displaystyle=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\text{\,.}

Combining the two bounds concludes the proof.
∎

Next, we show that the optimal value of the diagonal robust control problem is the maximum of the optimal scalar values, and that it is attained by the stacked mapping built from the scalar minimizers.

Corollary 3
.

Suppose
2
holds. Let
Q=V\diag({\mathbf{q}})V^{\top}\in\Theta_{V}
with
{\mathbf{q}}=(q_{1},\dots,q_{m})
, and for each
j\in[m]
define

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j}):=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{j}\in\mathbb{R}\\
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1\end{subarray}}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\text{\,.}

Then the optimal value of
Equation

13
among policies of the form
{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}):=K{\mathbf{x}}
with
K=V\diag({\mathbf{k}})V^{\top}
is given by

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\\
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1\ \forall j\in[m]\end{subarray}}\sup_{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\top}Q{\mathbf{x}}_{t}+{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})^{\top}R{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{x}}_{t})}{\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{w}}_{t}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}}=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Moreover, if for each
j
one chooses the scalar minimizer
k_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
and defines the stacked mapping

\displaystyle K^{*}(Q):=V\diag\left(k_{1}^{*}(q_{1}),\dots,k_{m}^{*}(q_{m})\right)V^{\top}\text{\,,}

then
K^{*}(Q)
attains the above value.

Proof.

By
Proposition

3
, for any diagonal controller matrix
K=V\diag({\mathbf{k}})V^{\top}
satisfying
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1
for all
j\in[m]
, the corresponding normalized robust objective equals

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\text{\,.}

Therefore the diagonal-controller optimization problem reduces to

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\\
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1\ \forall j\in[m]\end{subarray}}\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\text{\,.}

We claim this value is exactly
\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
.
For the lower bound, fix any
{\mathbf{k}}
with
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1
for all
j
. Then for every
j\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\geq\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,,}

and hence

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\geq\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Taking the infimum over
{\mathbf{k}}
gives

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\\
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1\ \forall j\end{subarray}}\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\geq\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

For the reverse inequality, let
k_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
be a scalar minimizer of
\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},\cdot)
for each
j\in[m]
, so that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j}^{*}(q_{j}))=\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Then the stacked mapping value
K^{*}(Q)=V\diag(k_{1}^{*}(q_{1}),\dots,k_{m}^{*}(q_{m}))V^{\top}
satisfies

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j}^{*}(q_{j}))=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\\
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1\ \forall j\end{subarray}}\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}(q_{j},k_{j})\leq\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Combining the two inequalities proves the claimed identity, and the same argument shows that the stacked mapping value
K^{*}(Q)
attains the optimum.
∎

C.3
DGARE Mapping and Active Index Coincidence

We now compare the stacked mapping
K^{*}(\cdot)
with the mapping
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(\cdot)
synthesized by the coupled block DGARE.
In this subsection, we first show that both mappings attain the same optimal value.
We then show that when the active scalar index,
i.e.
, the index attaining the maximum of the optimal scalar values, is unique, the coupled block DGARE mapping and the stacked mapping coincide on that active coordinate.

Corollary 4
.

Assume
2
. Let
Q=V\diag({\mathbf{q}})V^{\top}\in\Theta_{V}
with
{\mathbf{q}}=(q_{1},\dots,q_{m})
, and define
\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
and the stacked mapping value
K^{*}(Q)
as in
Corollary

3
.
Write

\displaystyle K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)=V\diag\left(k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{1}({\mathbf{q}}),\dots,k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{m}({\mathbf{q}})\right)V^{\top}\text{\,,}

as in
Proposition

1
.
Then

\displaystyle\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Moreover, both the stacked mapping
K^{*}(Q)
and the DGARE-synthesized mapping
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
attain this value, and in particular

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}\left(q_{j},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}})\right)=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})=\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}\text{\,.}

Proof.

By
Corollary

3
, the stacked mapping value
K^{*}(Q)
attains the value

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Since
\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}
is the minimum of the normalized robust objective over all policies by
Equation

15
, it follows that

\displaystyle\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}\leq\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

On the other hand, by
Equation

20
and the discussion following
Equation

19
, the DGARE-synthesized mapping value
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
attains the optimal value
\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}
and is stabilizing.
By
Proposition

1
,
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
is diagonal in the basis
V
, hence the closed-loop matrix
A+BK_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
is diagonal in this basis with diagonal entries
a_{j}+b_{j}k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}})
.
Since
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
is stabilizing, all eigenvalues of
A+BK_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
have modulus strictly smaller than
1
, and therefore

\displaystyle|a_{j}+b_{j}k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}})|<1

for all
j\in[m]
.
Hence
Proposition

3
implies that the value attained by
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
is

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}\left(q_{j},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}})\right)\text{\,.}

Moreover,
Corollary

3
yields

\displaystyle\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}\left(q_{j},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}})\right)\geq\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Combining this with the previous inequality gives

\displaystyle\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Substituting this equality into the previous display yields

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}\left(q_{j},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}})\right)=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})=\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}\text{\,.}

This proves the claim, and shows that both
K^{*}(Q)
and
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
attain the same optimal value.
∎

Lemma 10
.

Let
Q^{(0)}=V\diag({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)})V^{\top}\in\Theta_{V}
with
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
in the interior of
{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
.
Suppose there exists a unique index
j^{*}\in[m]
such that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}}^{(0)})=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j}^{(0)})\text{\,,}

which satisfies
q_{j^{*}}^{(0)}\in\left[0,\frac{|a_{j^{*}}|r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}{b_{j^{*}}^{2}}\right)
,
a_{j^{*}}\neq 0
, and
b_{j^{*}}\neq 0
.
Then there exists a neighborhood
{\mathcal{N}}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
of
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
such that for every
{\mathbf{q}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
the index
j^{*}
remains the unique active index, that is,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,,}

the DGARE-synthesized and stacked mapping values attain the same value on the active coordinate,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}\left(q_{j^{*}},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})\right)=\Gamma_{j^{*}}\left(q_{j^{*}},k_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})\right)=\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}\text{\,,}

and the unique minimizers coincide, meaning that for
Q=V\diag({\mathbf{q}})V^{\top}
, the DGARE-synthesized mapping value
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
and the stacked mapping value
K^{*}(Q)
satisfy

\displaystyle k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})=k_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=-\frac{a_{j^{*}}}{|a_{j^{*}}|}\frac{b_{j^{*}}}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}q_{j^{*}}\text{\,.}

Proof.

Fix any
j\in[m]
.
By
Lemma

9
, for any
q_{j}\in[0,1]
, the scalar robust control problem for which
\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
is the optimal value is given by

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{j}\in\mathbb{R}\\
|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|<1\end{subarray}}d_{j}^{2}\frac{q_{j}+r_{j}k_{j}^{2}}{(1-|a_{j}+b_{j}k_{j}|)^{2}}\text{\,.}

Therefore, the mapping
q_{j}\mapsto\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
is the infimum of affine functions of
q_{j}
. Hence it is concave on
[0,1]
and therefore continuous on
[0,1]
.
Next, since
j^{*}
is the unique maximizing index of
\left\{\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j}^{(0)})\right\}_{j\in[m]}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Delta:=\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}}^{(0)})-\max_{j\in[m]\setminus\{j^{*}\}}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j}^{(0)})>0\text{\,.}

Since
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
lies in the interior of
{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
and
q_{j^{*}}^{(0)}<\frac{|a_{j^{*}}|r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}{b_{j^{*}}^{2}}
, there exists a neighborhood
{\mathcal{N}}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
of
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
such that for every
{\mathbf{q}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
it holds that
q_{j^{*}}\in\left(0,\frac{|a_{j^{*}}|r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}{b_{j^{*}}^{2}}\right)
.
By continuity of each map
q_{j}\mapsto\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})
at
q_{j}^{(0)}
, after possibly shrinking
{\mathcal{N}}
we may assume that for every
{\mathbf{q}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
and every
j\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\left|\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})-\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j}^{(0)})\right|<\frac{\Delta}{3}\text{\,.}

Hence for every
{\mathbf{q}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
and every
j\neq j^{*}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})

\displaystyle>\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}}^{(0)})-\frac{\Delta}{3}

\displaystyle=\max_{\ell\in[m]\setminus\{j^{*}\}}\Gamma_{\ell}^{*}(q_{\ell}^{(0)})+\frac{2\Delta}{3}

\displaystyle\geq\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j}^{(0)})+\frac{2\Delta}{3}

\displaystyle>\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})+\frac{\Delta}{3}

\displaystyle>\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Therefore, for every
{\mathbf{q}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
, the index
j^{*}
remains the unique active index:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})\text{\,.}

Fix
{\mathbf{q}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
, and write
Q=V\diag({\mathbf{q}})V^{\top}
. Then
Corollary

4
applies to this
Q
.
Since
j^{*}
is the unique active index,
Corollary

4
yields

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}\left(q_{j},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}})\right)=\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}^{*}(q_{j})=\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}\text{\,.}

On the other hand, by definition of the scalar optimum,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}\left(q_{j^{*}},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})\right)\geq\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})\text{\,.}

Since the
j^{*}
th term is one of the terms in the maximum, the previous display also implies that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}\left(q_{j^{*}},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})\right)\leq\max_{j\in[m]}\Gamma_{j}\left(q_{j},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j}({\mathbf{q}})\right)=\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})\text{\,.}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}\left(q_{j^{*}},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})\right)=\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}\text{\,.}

By
Proposition

2
, the scalar objective
\Gamma_{j^{*}}(q_{j^{*}},\cdot)
has the unique minimizer

\displaystyle k_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=-\frac{a_{j^{*}}}{|a_{j^{*}}|}\frac{b_{j^{*}}}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}q_{j^{*}}\text{\,.}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}\left(q_{j^{*}},k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})\right)=\Gamma_{j^{*}}\left(q_{j^{*}},k_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})\right)=\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=\gamma_{\infty}(Q)^{2}\text{\,.}

Since both
k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})
and
k_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})
attain the minimum value of the same scalar objective, uniqueness of the minimizer implies

\displaystyle k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})=k_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=-\frac{a_{j^{*}}}{|a_{j^{*}}|}\frac{b_{j^{*}}}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}q_{j^{*}}\text{\,.}

∎

C.4
Lipschitz Constant of the
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
Mapping

We now turn the local coincidence from
Section

C.3
into a lower bound on the Lipschitz constant of the coupled block DGARE mapping.
The key step is to construct an interior diagonal cost matrix
Q^{(0)}
for which a prescribed coordinate is uniquely active.
On the corresponding neighborhood, the coupled block DGARE mapping and the stacked mapping coincide on that coordinate, so the DGARE entry inherits the explicit affine form of the stacked mapping.
This produces an explicit derivative, which
Lemma

8
converts into a lower bound on
\text{Lip}(K_{\mathrm{safe}})
.
In
Section

C.5
, we will apply the next result with the index
j^{*}
furnished by the alignment condition of
Definition

1
.

Proposition 4
.

Assume
2
. Fix an index
j^{*}\in[m]
such that
a_{j^{*}}\neq 0
,
b_{j^{*}}\neq 0
, and
d_{j^{*}}\neq 0
and define

\displaystyle\bar{q}_{j^{*}}:=\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{1,\frac{|a_{j^{*}}|r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}{b_{j^{*}}^{2}}\right\}\text{\,.}

Then there exists
\eta>0
such that, for
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
defined by

\displaystyle{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}:=\bar{q}_{j^{*}}{\mathbf{e}}_{j^{*}}+\eta\sum_{j\in[m]\setminus\{j^{*}\}}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\text{\,,}

the matrix
Q^{(0)}:=V\diag({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)})V^{\top}
belongs to
\Theta_{V}
, the vector
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
lies in the interior of
{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
, and
j^{*}
is the unique active index at
Q^{(0)}
.
Moreover, there exists a neighborhood
{\mathcal{N}}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
of
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
such that for every
{\mathbf{q}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
, the DGARE-synthesized mapping
K_{\mathrm{safe}}(Q)
and the stacked mapping
K^{*}(Q)
coincide on the coordinate
j^{*}
, meaning that

\displaystyle k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})=k_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=-\frac{a_{j^{*}}}{|a_{j^{*}}|}\frac{b_{j^{*}}}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}q_{j^{*}}\text{\,,}

and consequently

\displaystyle\text{Lip}(K_{\mathrm{safe}})\geq\frac{|b_{j^{*}}|}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}\text{\,.}

Proof.

By construction,
\bar{q}_{j^{*}}\in(0,\frac{|a_{j^{*}}|r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}{b_{j^{*}}^{2}})
.
Therefore since
a_{j^{*}}\neq 0
and
b_{j^{*}}\neq 0
,
Proposition

2
applies at the coordinate
j^{*}
and gives

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(\bar{q}_{j^{*}})=\frac{d_{j^{*}}^{2}}{(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)^{2}}\cdot\frac{\bar{q}_{j^{*}}}{1+\frac{b_{j^{*}}^{2}}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)^{2}}\bar{q}_{j^{*}}}>0\text{\,,}

where strict positivity uses
d_{j^{*}}\neq 0
and
\bar{q}_{j^{*}}>0
.
By continuity of each
\Gamma_{j}^{*}(\cdot)
on
[0,1]
, and by the fact that
\Gamma_{j}^{*}(0)=0
for every
j\in[m]
, there exists
\eta_{j^{*}}>0
small enough so that for every
j\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}^{*}(\eta_{j^{*}})<\Gamma_{j^{*}}^{*}(\bar{q}_{j^{*}})\text{\,,}

and we may additionally require that

\displaystyle\eta_{j^{*}}<\sqrt{\frac{1-\bar{q}_{j^{*}}^{2}}{m}}\text{\,.}

Then, defining
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
as described above with
\eta_{j^{*}}
, all coordinates of
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
are strictly positive and

\displaystyle\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}^{2}=\bar{q}_{j^{*}}^{2}+(m-1)\eta_{j^{*}}^{2}<\bar{q}_{j^{*}}^{2}+\frac{m-1}{m}(1-\bar{q}_{j^{*}}^{2})=\frac{m-1+\bar{q}_{j^{*}}^{2}}{m}<\frac{m-1+\frac{1}{2}}{m}<1\text{\,,}

where we used the fact that
\bar{q}_{j^{*}}<\frac{1}{2}
in the last inequality.
Hence
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
lies in the interior of
{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
, and therefore
Q^{(0)}\in\Theta_{V}
.
Thus,
Lemma

10
applies at
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
, and in particular there exists a neighborhood
{\mathcal{N}}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
of
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}
such that for every
{\mathbf{q}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
,

\displaystyle k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}})=k_{j^{*}}^{*}(q_{j^{*}})=-\frac{a_{j^{*}}}{|a_{j^{*}}|}\frac{b_{j^{*}}}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}q_{j^{*}}\text{\,.}

Concretely, for all sufficiently small
h\in\mathbb{R}
such that
{\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j^{*}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j^{*}})=-\frac{a_{j^{*}}}{|a_{j^{*}}|}\frac{b_{j^{*}}}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}(q_{j^{*}}^{(0)}+h)\text{\,.}

In particular, the scalar function
g_{j^{*}}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
defined for any
h\in\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle g_{j^{*}}(h):=k^{\mathrm{safe}}_{j^{*}}({\mathbf{q}}^{(0)}+h{\mathbf{e}}_{j^{*}})

is differentiable at
h=0
with derivative
g_{j^{*}}^{\prime}(0)=-\frac{a_{j^{*}}}{|a_{j^{*}}|}\frac{b_{j^{*}}}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}
.
Applying
Lemma

8
thus yields

\displaystyle\text{Lip}(K_{\mathrm{safe}})\geq|g_{j^{*}}^{\prime}(0)|=\frac{|b_{j^{*}}|}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}\text{\,,}

completing the proof.
∎

C.5
Comparing the Lipschitz Constants

We are now ready to conclude
Theorem

1
.
In this subsection, we compare the lower bound on the DGARE-synthesized mapping Lipschitz constant with the upper bound on the LQR mapping Lipschitz constant.
The theorem then follows by comparing the resulting dimensionless prefactors.

Let
\alpha\in[0,1]
be the alignment constant of the dynamical system, and let
j^{*}\in[m]
be a witnessing index as guaranteed by
3
. Rearranging the condition on
|a_{j^{*}}|
,

\displaystyle|a_{j^{*}}|\geq\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{A}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}+\alpha-1\right)=1-\frac{1-\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{A}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}{\alpha}\text{\,,}

we obtain that
1-|a_{j^{*}}|\leq\frac{1-\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{A}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}{\alpha}
.
Together with the conditions on
b_{j^{*}}
and
r_{j^{*}}
given by

\displaystyle|b_{j^{*}}|\geq\alpha\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{B}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\text{\,,}\qquad r_{j^{*}}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{R^{-1}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}\text{\,,}

yields

\displaystyle\text{Lip}(K_{\mathrm{safe}})\geq\frac{|b_{j^{*}}|}{r_{j^{*}}(1-|a_{j^{*}}|)}\geq\frac{\alpha\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox
